first visit of the target set, and (b) it uses the cumulative consumption
instead of resource levels to ignore the effect of reload states.

Given
T\subseteq S
and
i\in\mathbb{N}
, the objective

\mathsf{N}_{T}^{i}
(
bounded non-reloading reachability
) is the
set of all (not necessary safe) loaded runs

\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}s^{{\kern-6.43582pt{d}\kern 3.77432pt}}_{{\kern-12.04666pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.77432pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}s^{{\kern-6.43582pt{d}\kern 3.77432pt}}_{{\kern-12.04666pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.77432pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}s^{{\kern-4.22968pt{d}\kern 2.29686pt}}_{{\kern-8.23741pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.29686pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}s^{{\kern-3.57344pt{d}\kern 1.64063pt}}_{{\kern-7.58118pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.64063pt}}}_{1}a_{1}s_{2}a_{2}\ldots
such that for some
1\leq f\leq i+1
it
holds
s_{f}\in T
and
\sum_{j=1}^{f-1}\gamma(s_{j},a_{j})\leq d
. The union

\mathsf{N}_{T}=\bigcup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\mathsf{N}_{T}^{i}
forms the

non-reloading reachability
objective.

Let us now fix some
T\subseteq S
. In the next few paragraphs, we
discuss the solution of sure non-reloading reachability of
T
:
computing the vector
\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}_{T}]
and the corresponding
witness strategy. The solution is based on backward induction (with respect to
number of steps needed to reach
T
). The key concept here is the

value of action

a
in a state
s
based on a vector

\mathbf{v}\in\overline{\mathbb{N}}^{S\hskip-3.53336pt}
, denoted as
\mathit{AV}(\mathbf{v},s,a)
and
defined as follows.

\mathit{AV}(\mathbf{v},s,a)=\gamma(s,a)+\max_{t\in\mathit{Succ}(s,a)}\mathbf{v}(t)

Intuitively,
\mathit{AV}(\mathbf{v},s,a)
is the consumption of
a
in
s
plus the
worst value of
\mathbf{v}
among the relevant successors. Now imagine that

\mathbf{v}
is equal to
\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}^{i}]
; that is, for each
state
s
it contains the minimal amount of resource needed (without reloading)
to reach
T
in at most
i
steps. Then,
\mathit{AV}
for
a
in
s
is the
minimal amount of resource needed to reach
T
in
i+1
steps when playing

a
in
s
.

The following functional
\mathcal{F}\colon\overline{\mathbb{N}}^{S}\to\overline{\mathbb{N}}^{S}
is a simple generalization of the standard Bellman
functional used for computing shortest paths in graphs. We use

\mathcal{F}^{i}(\mathbf{v})
for the result of
i
applications of
\mathcal{F}

on
\mathbf{v}
.

\mathcal{F}(\mathbf{v})(s)=\begin{cases}0&s\in T\\
\min_{a\in A}\mathit{AV}(\mathbf{v},s,a)&s\not\in T\\
\end{cases}

To complete our induction-based computation, we need to find the right
initialization vector
\mathbf{x}_{T}
for
\mathcal{F}
. As the intuition
for action value hints,
\mathbf{x}_{T}
should be precisely

\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}^{0}]
and thus is defined as

\mathbf{x}_{T}(s)=0
for
s\in T
and as

\mathbf{x}_{T}(s)=\infty
otherwise.

Lemma 1
.

It holds that

\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}_{T}^{i}]=\mathcal{F}^{i}(\mathbf{x}_{T})

for every
i\geq 0
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
i
. The base case for
i=0
is trivial. Now assume
that the lemma holds for some
i\geq 0
.

From the definition of
\mathsf{N}^{i}
we have that a loaded run
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathit{\varrho}^{{\kern-6.81049pt{d}\kern 4.14899pt}}_{{\kern-12.42133pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.14899pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathit{\varrho}^{{\kern-6.81049pt{d}\kern 4.14899pt}}_{{\kern-12.42133pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.14899pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathit{\varrho}^{{\kern-4.46619pt{d}\kern 2.53337pt}}_{{\kern-8.47392pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.53337pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathit{\varrho}^{{\kern-3.74236pt{d}\kern 1.80954pt}}_{{\kern-7.75009pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.80954pt}}}=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}s^{{\kern-6.43582pt{d}\kern 3.77432pt}}_{{\kern-12.04666pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.77432pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}s^{{\kern-6.43582pt{d}\kern 3.77432pt}}_{{\kern-12.04666pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.77432pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}s^{{\kern-4.22968pt{d}\kern 2.29686pt}}_{{\kern-8.23741pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.29686pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}s^{{\kern-3.57344pt{d}\kern 1.64063pt}}_{{\kern-7.58118pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.64063pt}}}_{1}a_{1}s_{2}\ldots
satisfies
surely
\mathsf{N}^{i+1}
if and only if
s_{1}\in T
or

\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{h}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathit{\varrho}^{{\kern-7.08328pt{h}\kern 4.14899pt}}_{{\kern-13.29428pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.14899pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{h}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathit{\varrho}^{{\kern-7.08328pt{h}\kern 4.14899pt}}_{{\kern-13.29428pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.14899pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{h}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathit{\varrho}^{{\kern-4.66104pt{h}\kern 2.53337pt}}_{{\kern-9.09746pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.53337pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{h}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathit{\varrho}^{{\kern-3.93721pt{h}\kern 1.80954pt}}_{{\kern-8.37363pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.80954pt}}}_{2..}\in\mathsf{N}^{i}
for
h=d-\gamma(s_{1},a_{1})
.
Therefore, given a state
s\notin T
and the load

d=\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}^{i+1}](s)
, each witness strategy
\sigma

for
\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}^{i+1}]
must guarantee that if

\sigma(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}s^{{\kern-6.43582pt{d}\kern 3.77432pt}}_{{\kern-12.04666pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.77432pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}s^{{\kern-6.43582pt{d}\kern 3.77432pt}}_{{\kern-12.04666pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.77432pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}s^{{\kern-4.22968pt{d}\kern 2.29686pt}}_{{\kern-8.23741pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.29686pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}s^{{\kern-3.57344pt{d}\kern 1.64063pt}}_{{\kern-7.58118pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.64063pt}}})=a
then
d\geq\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}^{i}](s^{\prime})+\gamma(s,a)
for all
s^{\prime}\in\mathit{Succ}(s,a)
. That is,

d\geq\mathit{AV}(\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}^{i}],s,a)=\mathit{AV}(\mathcal{F}^{i}(\mathbf{x}_{T}),s,a)
.

On the other hand, let
a_{m}
be the action with minimal
AV
for
s
based on

\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}^{i}]
. The strategy that plays
a_{m}
in the first step and then mimics some witness strategy for
\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}^{i}]

surely satisfies
\mathsf{N}^{i+1}
from
s
loaded by
\mathit{AV}(\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}^{i}],s,a_{m})
. Therefore,
d\leq\mathit{AV}(\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}^{i}],s,a_{m})=\mathit{AV}(\mathcal{F}^{i}(\mathbf{x}_{T}),s,a_{m})
.

Together,
d=\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}^{i+1}](s)=\min_{a\in A}\mathit{AV}(\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}^{i}],s,a)=\mathcal{F}(\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}^{i}])(s)
and that is by induction
hypothesis equal to
\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{F}^{i}(\mathbf{x}_{T}))(s)=\mathcal{F}^{i+1}(\mathbf{x}_{T})(s)
.
∎

Theorem 1
.

Denote by
n
the length of the longest simple path
in
\mathcal{C}
. Iterating
\mathcal{F}
on
\mathbf{x}_{T}
yields a fixed point in at most

n
steps and this fixed point equals
\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}_{T}]
.

Proof.

For the sake of contradiction, suppose that
\mathcal{F}
does not yield a
fixed point after
n
steps. Then there exists a state
s
such that
d=\mathcal{F}^{n+1}(\mathbf{x}_{T})(s)<\mathcal{F}^{n}(\mathbf{x}_{T})(s)
. Let
\sigma
be a witness
strategy for
\mathcal{F}^{n+1}(\mathbf{x}_{T})=\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}^{n+1}]
. Now let
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathit{\varrho}^{{\kern-6.81049pt{d}\kern 4.14899pt}}_{{\kern-12.42133pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.14899pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathit{\varrho}^{{\kern-6.81049pt{d}\kern 4.14899pt}}_{{\kern-12.42133pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.14899pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathit{\varrho}^{{\kern-4.46619pt{d}\kern 2.53337pt}}_{{\kern-8.47392pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.53337pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathit{\varrho}^{{\kern-3.74236pt{d}\kern 1.80954pt}}_{{\kern-7.75009pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.80954pt}}}=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}s^{{\kern-6.43582pt{d}\kern 3.77432pt}}_{{\kern-12.04666pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.77432pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}s^{{\kern-6.43582pt{d}\kern 3.77432pt}}_{{\kern-12.04666pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.77432pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}s^{{\kern-4.22968pt{d}\kern 2.29686pt}}_{{\kern-8.23741pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.29686pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}s^{{\kern-3.57344pt{d}\kern 1.64063pt}}_{{\kern-7.58118pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.64063pt}}}_{1}a_{1}s_{2}\ldots
be a loaded run from

\mathsf{Comp}(\sigma,s,d)
such that
s_{i}\notin T
for all
i\leq n+1
and
s_{n+2}\in T
, and such that for all
1\leq k\leq n+1
it
holds
d-c_{k}=\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}^{n+1-k}](s_{k+1})
where
c_{k}=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\gamma(s_{j},a_{j})
is the consumption of the first
k

actions of
\mathit{\varrho}
. Such a run must exist, otherwise some

\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}^{i}]
can be improved.

As
n
is the length of the longest simple path in
\mathcal{C}
, we can conclude
that there are two indices
f<l\leq n+1
such that
s_{f}=s_{l}=t
. But
since
\mathcal{C}
is decreasing, we have that
c_{f}<c_{l}
and thus

\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}^{n+1-f}](t)=d-c_{f}>d-c_{l}=\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}^{n+1-l}](t)
. As
n+1-f>n+1-l
, we reached a
contradiction with the fact that
\mathsf{N}^{n+1-f}\supseteq\mathsf{N}^{n+1-l}
.

By
Lemma

1
we have that
\mathcal{F}^{n}(\mathbf{x}_{T})=\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}]
.
∎

Witness strategy for
\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}]
.
Any memoryless strategy

\sigma
that picks for each history ending with a state
s
some action
a_{s}

such that
\mathit{AV}(\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}],s,a_{s})=\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}](s)
is clearly a witness strategy for

\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}]
, which is,

\sigma\!\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}]}}}}\models^{{\kern-20.09148pt{\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}]}\kern 7.52623pt{\!}}}_{{\kern-7.13734pt\kern 7.52623pt{}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}]}}}}\models^{{\kern-20.09148pt{\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}]}\kern 7.52623pt{\!}}}_{{\kern-7.13734pt\kern 7.52623pt{}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}]}}}}\models^{{\kern-14.95811pt{\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}]}\kern 5.95122pt{\!}}}_{{\kern-5.56233pt\kern 5.95122pt{}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}]}}}}\models^{{\kern-13.90813pt{\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}]}\kern 4.90123pt{\!}}}_{{\kern-4.51234pt\kern 4.90123pt{}}}}\!\mathsf{N}
.

IV-B

Safely reaching reloads from reloads

The objective
\mathsf{N}
is sufficient for the property (ii) with

T=\mathit{R}^{\prime}
. But we cannot use it off-the-shelf to guarantee the property
(i) at most
\mathit{cap}
units of resource are consumed between two consecutive
visits of
\mathit{R}^{\prime}\hskip-2.22223pt
. For that, we need to solve the problem of

reachability within at least
1
steps
(starting in
T
alone
does not count as reaching
T
here). We define

\mathsf{N}_{+T}^{i}
in the same way as
\mathsf{N}_{T}
but we
enforce that
f>1
and we set

\mathsf{N}_{+T}=\bigcup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\mathsf{N}_{+T}
. To
compute
\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}_{+T}]
, we slightly alter
\mathcal{F}

using the following truncation operator.

\llfloor\,\mathbf{v}\,\rrfloor_{T}(s)=\begin{cases}\mathbf{v}(s)&\text{if }s\not\in T,\\
0&\text{if }s\in T.\\
\end{cases}

The new functional
\mathcal{G}
applied to
\mathbf{v}
computes the new value
in the same way for all states (including states from
T
), but treats

\mathbf{v}(t)
as
0
for
t\in T
.

\mathcal{G}(\mathbf{v})(s)=\min\nolimits_{a\in A}\mathit{AV}(\llfloor\,\mathbf{v}\,\rrfloor_{T},s,a)

Let
\boldsymbol{\infty}^{S}\in\overline{\mathbb{N}}^{S}
denote the vector with all components equal to

\infty
. Clearly,
\llfloor\,\boldsymbol{\infty}^{S}\,\rrfloor_{T}=\mathbf{x}_{T}
, and thus it
is easy to see that for all
s\notin T
and
i\in\mathbb{N}
we have that

\mathcal{G}^{i}(\boldsymbol{\infty}^{S})(s)=\mathcal{F}^{i}(\mathbf{x}_{T})(s)
. Moreover,

\llfloor\,\mathcal{G}^{i}(\boldsymbol{\infty}^{S})\,\rrfloor_{T}=\mathcal{F}^{i}(\mathbf{x}_{T})
. A
slight modification of arguments used to prove
Lemma

1
and

Theorem

1
shows that
\mathcal{G}
indeed computes

\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}_{+T}]
and we need at most
n+1
iterations for the
desired fixed point.
Algorithm

1
iteratively applies
\mathcal{G}
on
\boldsymbol{\infty}^{S}
until a fixed point is reached.

Input:

CMDP
\mathcal{C}=(S,A,\Delta,\gamma,\mathit{R},\mathit{cap})
and
T\subseteq S

Output:

The vector
\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}_{+T}]_{\mathcal{C}}

\mathbf{v}\leftarrow\boldsymbol{\infty}^{S}
;

1

repeat

2

\mathbf{v}_{\mathit{old}}\leftarrow\mathbf{v}
;

3

foreach

s\in S

do

4

c\leftarrow\min_{a\in A}\mathit{AV}(\llfloor\,\mathbf{v}_{\mathit{old}}\,\rrfloor_{T},s,a)
;

5

if

c<\mathbf{v}(s)

then

6

\mathbf{v}(s)\leftarrow c
;

7

until

\,\mathbf{v}_{\mathit{old}}=\mathbf{v}
;

8

return

\mathbf{v}

Algorithm 1

Computing
\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}_{+T}]
.

Theorem 2
.

Given a CMDP
\mathcal{C}
and a set of target states

T
,
Algorithm

1
computes the vector

\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}_{+T}]_{\mathcal{C}}
. Moreover, the repeat-loop terminates
after at most
|S|
iterations.

Proof.

Each iteration of the repeat-loop computes an application of
\mathcal{G}
on
the value of
\mathbf{v}
from line
3
(stored in
\mathbf{v}_{\mathit{old}}
) and stores the
resulting values in
\mathbf{v}
on line
7
. Iterating
\mathcal{G}
on

\boldsymbol{\infty}^{S}
yields a fixed point in at most
n+1
iterations where
n
is
the length of the longest simple path in
\mathcal{C}
. As
n+1\leq|S|
, the
test on line 8 becomes true after at most
|S|
iterations and
\mathbf{v}

on line
8
contains the result of
\mathcal{G}^{i}(\boldsymbol{\infty}^{S})
where
i

is the actual number of iterations. Thus, the value of
\mathbf{v}
on line
9

is equal to
\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}_{+T}]_{\mathcal{C}}
and is computed in at most

|S|
iterations.
∎

Now with
Algorithm

1
we can compute

\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}_{+\mathit{R}}]_{\mathcal{C}}
and see which reload states should be
avoided by safe runs: the reloads that need more than
\mathit{cap}
units
of resource to surely reach
\mathit{R}
again. We call such reload states

unusable in
\mathcal{C}
. w

IV-C

Detecting useful reloads and solving the safety problem

Using
Algorithm

1
, we can identify reload states that
are unusable in
\mathcal{C}
. However, it does not automatically mean that the rest
of the reload states form the desired set
\mathit{R}^{\prime}
for property (i).
Consider the CMDP
\mathcal{D}
in
Fig.

2
. The
only reload state that is unusable is
w

(
\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}_{+\mathit{R}}](w)=\infty
). But clearly, all
runs that avoid
w
must avoid
v
and
x
as well. This intuition is backed
up by the fact that
\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}_{+\mathit{R}_{1}}](v)=\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}_{+\mathit{R}_{1}}](x)=\infty
for
\mathit{R}_{1}=\mathit{R}\smallsetminus\{w\}=\{u,v,x\}
, see

Fig.

3
. The property (i) indeed translates to

\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}_{+\mathit{R}^{\prime}}](r)\leq\mathit{cap}
for all

r\in\mathit{R}^{\prime}
; naturally, we want to identify the maximal

\mathit{R}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathit{R}
for which this holds.
Algorithm

2
finds
the desired
\mathit{R}^{\prime}
by iteratively removing unusable
reloads from the current candidate set
\mathit{R}^{\prime}
until there is no unusable
reload in
\mathit{R}^{\prime}
(lines 3-7).

.

With the right set
\mathit{R}^{\prime}
in hand, we can move on to the property (ii) of
safe runs: navigating safely towards reloads in
\mathit{R}^{\prime}
, which equals to
the objective
\mathsf{N}_{\mathit{R}^{\prime}}
from
Section

IV-A
. We can
reuse
Algorithm

1
for it as
\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}]=\llfloor\,\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}_{+}]\,\rrfloor
regardless the target set. Based on
properties (i) and (ii), we claim that
\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}]=\llfloor\,\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}_{+\mathit{R}^{\prime}}]\,\rrfloor_{\mathit{R}^{\prime}}
. Indeed, we need at most

\mathit{cap}
units of resource to move between reloads of
\mathit{R}^{\prime}
, and we need at
most
\llfloor\,\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}_{+\mathit{R}^{\prime}}]\,\rrfloor_{\mathit{R}^{\prime}}(s)
units
of resource to reach
\mathit{R}^{\prime}
from
s
.

Whenever
\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}](s)>\mathit{cap}
for some state
s
, the exact
value is not important for us; the meaning is still that there is no strategy

\sigma
and no initial load
d\leq\mathit{cap}
such that

\sigma\!\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{{s}}}}\models^{{\kern-10.18773pt{d}\kern 7.52623pt{\!}}}_{{\kern-9.9342pt{s}\kern 7.52623pt{}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{{s}}}}\models^{{\kern-10.18773pt{d}\kern 7.52623pt{\!}}}_{{\kern-9.9342pt{s}\kern 7.52623pt{}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{{s}}}}\models^{{\kern-7.88403pt{d}\kern 5.95122pt{\!}}}_{{\kern-7.70296pt{s}\kern 5.95122pt{}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{{s}}}}\models^{{\kern-6.83405pt{d}\kern 4.90123pt{\!}}}_{{\kern-6.65297pt{s}\kern 4.90123pt{}}}}\!\mathsf{S}
. Hence, we can set

\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}](s)=\infty
in all such cases. To achieve this, we
extend the operator
\llfloor\,\cdot\,\rrfloor_{T}
into

\llbracket\,\cdot\,\rrbracket_{T}^{\mathit{cap}}
as follows.

\llbracket\,\mathbf{x}\,\rrbracket_{T}^{\mathit{cap}}(s)=\begin{cases}\infty&\text{if }\mathbf{x}(s)>\mathit{cap}\\
\mathbf{x}(s)&\text{if }\mathbf{x}(s)\leq\mathit{cap}\text{ and }s\not\in T\\
0&\text{if }\mathbf{x}(s)\leq\mathit{cap}\text{ and }s\in T\\
\end{cases}

Input:

CMDP
\mathcal{C}=(S,A,\Delta,\gamma,\mathit{R},\mathit{cap})

Output:

The vector
\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}]_{\mathcal{C}}

\mathit{R}^{\prime}\leftarrow\mathit{R}
;

1

\mathit{Unusable}\leftarrow\emptyset
;

2

repeat

3

\mathit{R}^{\prime}\leftarrow\mathit{R}^{\prime}\smallsetminus\mathit{Unusable}
;

4

\mathbf{n}\leftarrow\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}_{+\mathit{R}^{\prime}}]_{\mathcal{C}}
;

/*
Algorithm

1
with
T=\mathit{R}^{\prime}
*/

\mathit{Unusable}\leftarrow\{r\in\mathit{R}^{\prime}\mid\mathbf{n}(r)>\mathit{cap}\}
;

5

until

\mathit{Unusable}=\emptyset
;

6

return

\llbracket\,\mathbf{n}\,\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}^{\prime}}^{\mathit{cap}}
;

7

Algorithm 2

Computing
\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}]
.

Theorem 3
.

Algorithm
2
computes the vector
\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}]_{\mathcal{C}}
in
time polynomial with respect to the representation of
\mathcal{C}
.

Proof.

Complexity.
The algorithm clearly terminates. Computing

\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}_{+\mathit{R}^{\prime}}]
on line
2
takes a
polynomial number of steps per call (
Theorem

2
). Since the
repeat loop performs at most
|\mathit{R}|
iterations, the complexity follows.

Correctness.
We first prove that upon termination

\llbracket\,\mathbf{n}\,\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}^{\prime}}^{\mathit{cap}}(s)\leq\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}]_{\mathcal{C}}(s)
for each
s\in S
whenever the latter
value is finite. This is implied by the fact that

\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}_{+\mathit{R}^{\prime}}]\leq\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}]
is the invariant of the
algorithm. To see that, it suffices to show that at every point of execution,

\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}](t)=\infty
for each
t\in\mathit{R}\smallsetminus\mathit{R}^{\prime}
: if this holds, each strategy that satisfies
\mathsf{S}
must avoid
states in
\mathit{R}\smallsetminus\mathit{R}^{\prime}
(due to property (i) of safe runs)
and thus the first reload on runs compatible with such a strategy must be from

\mathit{R}^{\prime}
.

Let
\mathit{R}^{\prime}_{i}
denote the contents of
\mathit{R}^{\prime}
after the
i
-th iteration.
We prove, by induction on
i
, that
\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}](t)=\infty
for
all
t\in\mathit{R}\smallsetminus\mathit{R}^{\prime}
. For
i=0
we have
\mathit{R}=\mathit{R}^{\prime}_{0}
, so the statement holds. For
i>0
, let
t\in\mathit{R}\smallsetminus\mathit{R}^{\prime}_{i}
, then it must exist some
j<i
such that

\mathbf{n}(t)=\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}_{+\mathit{R}^{\prime}_{j}}](t)>\mathit{cap}
, hence no strategy can safely reach
\mathit{R}^{\prime}_{j}
from
t
and by induction
hypothesis, the reload states from
\mathit{R}\smallsetminus\mathit{R}^{\prime}_{j}
must be
avoided by strategies that satisfy
\mathsf{S}
. Together, as
\mathcal{C}
is
decreasing, there is no strategy
\sigma
such that

\sigma\!\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathit{cap}}}}_{{{t}}}}\models^{{\kern-14.69572pt{\mathit{cap}}\kern 7.52623pt{\!}}}_{{\kern-9.40678pt{t}\kern 7.52623pt{}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathit{cap}}}}_{{{t}}}}\models^{{\kern-14.69572pt{\mathit{cap}}\kern 7.52623pt{\!}}}_{{\kern-9.40678pt{t}\kern 7.52623pt{}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathit{cap}}}}_{{{t}}}}\models^{{\kern-11.09674pt{\mathit{cap}}\kern 5.95122pt{\!}}}_{{\kern-7.32622pt{t}\kern 5.95122pt{}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathit{cap}}}}_{{{t}}}}\models^{{\kern-10.04675pt{\mathit{cap}}\kern 4.90123pt{\!}}}_{{\kern-6.27623pt{t}\kern 4.90123pt{}}}}\!\mathsf{S}
and hence
\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}](t)=\infty
.

Finally, we need to prove that upon termination,
\llbracket\,\mathbf{n}\,\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}^{\prime}}^{\mathit{cap}}\geq\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}]
. As
\mathbf{n}=\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{N}_{+\mathit{R}^{\prime}}]
and

\mathbf{n}(r)\leq\mathit{cap}
for each
r\in\mathit{R}^{\prime}
, then, for each
s

with
d=\llbracket\,\mathbf{n}\,\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}^{\prime}}^{\mathit{cap}}(s)\leq\mathit{cap}
there exists a
strategy that can reach
\mathit{R}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathit{R}
consuming at most
d

units of resource, and, once in
\mathit{R}^{\prime}
,
\sigma
can always return to

\mathit{R}^{\prime}
within
\mathit{cap}
units of resource. Thus, all runs in

\mathsf{Comp}(\sigma,s,d)
are safe and
d
is enough for
\mathsf{S}
in

s
.
∎

IV-D

Safe strategies

Definition 4
.

Let
s\in S
be a state and let
0\leq d\leq\mathit{cap}
be a
resource level. We call an action
a

safe
in
s
with
d
if (1)

v=\mathit{AV}(\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}],s,a)\leq d
, or if (2)
v\leq\mathit{cap}
and

s\in\mathit{R}
, or if (3)
\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}](s)>\mathit{cap}
. Further,
a
is

min-safe
in
s
if it is safe in
s
with

d=\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}](s)
. We call a strategy
\sigma

safe
if it
picks an action that is safe in the current state with the current resource
level whenever possible.

Remarks.
By definition, no action is safe in
s
for all
r<\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}](s)<\infty
(otherwise
\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}](s)

is at most
r
). On the other hand, there is always at least one action that
is min-safe for each state
s
and, in particular, all actions are safe and
min-safe in
s
with
\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}](s)=\infty
.

Lemma 2
.

Let
\sigma
be a safe strategy. Then

\sigma\!\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}]}}}}\models^{{\kern-19.34286pt{\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}]}\kern 7.52623pt{\!}}}_{{\kern-7.13734pt\kern 7.52623pt{}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}]}}}}\models^{{\kern-19.34286pt{\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}]}\kern 7.52623pt{\!}}}_{{\kern-7.13734pt\kern 7.52623pt{}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}]}}}}\models^{{\kern-14.42339pt{\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}]}\kern 5.95122pt{\!}}}_{{\kern-5.56233pt\kern 5.95122pt{}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}]}}}}\models^{{\kern-13.3734pt{\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}]}\kern 4.90123pt{\!}}}_{{\kern-4.51234pt\kern 4.90123pt{}}}}\!\mathsf{S}
.

Proof.

We need to prove that, given a state
s
with
\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}](s)\leq\mathit{cap}
and an initial load
d
such that
\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}](s)\leq d\leq\mathit{cap}
, all runs in
\mathsf{Comp}(\sigma,s,d)
are safe. To do this, we
show that all
s
-initiated
d
-loaded paths
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\alpha^{{\kern-7.86026pt{d}\kern 5.19876pt}}_{{\kern-13.4711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.19876pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\alpha^{{\kern-7.86026pt{d}\kern 5.19876pt}}_{{\kern-13.4711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.19876pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\alpha^{{\kern-5.06735pt{d}\kern 3.13454pt}}_{{\kern-9.07509pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.13454pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\alpha^{{\kern-4.17177pt{d}\kern 2.23895pt}}_{{\kern-8.1795pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.23895pt}}}
created
by
\sigma
are safe. By simple induction with respect to the length of the
path we prove that
\mathit{last}\mathit{RL}(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\alpha^{{\kern-7.86026pt{d}\kern 5.19876pt}}_{{\kern-13.4711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.19876pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\alpha^{{\kern-7.86026pt{d}\kern 5.19876pt}}_{{\kern-13.4711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.19876pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\alpha^{{\kern-5.06735pt{d}\kern 3.13454pt}}_{{\kern-9.07509pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.13454pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\alpha^{{\kern-4.17177pt{d}\kern 2.23895pt}}_{{\kern-8.1795pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.23895pt}}})\geq\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}](t)\neq\bot
where

. For

and all

8.6463pt}}_{{\kern-18.89682pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.01288pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2842pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.6463pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d^{\prime}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.01288pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2842pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}s^{\prime}as^{{\kern-9.9891pt{d^{\prime}}\kern 6.5938pt}}_{{\kern-16.84431pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.01288pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2842pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.5938pt}}})=d^{\prime}-\gamma(s^{\prime},a)\geq\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}](s)
by
definition of action value and safe actions, and thus by the induction
hypothesis we have that
\mathit{last}\mathit{RL}(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{d^{\prime}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.01288pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2842pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}s^{\prime}as\odot\alpha^{{\kern-32.94852pt{d^{\prime}}\kern 28.82452pt}}_{{\kern-42.33081pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.01288pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2842pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 28.82452pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d^{\prime}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.01288pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2842pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}s^{\prime}as\odot\alpha^{{\kern-32.94852pt{d^{\prime}}\kern 28.82452pt}}_{{\kern-42.33081pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.01288pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2842pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 28.82452pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d^{\prime}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.01288pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2842pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}s^{\prime}as\odot\alpha^{{\kern-22.62923pt{d^{\prime}}\kern 19.23392pt}}_{{\kern-29.48444pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.01288pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2842pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 19.23392pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d^{\prime}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.01288pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2842pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}s^{\prime}as\odot\alpha^{{\kern-18.59225pt{d^{\prime}}\kern 15.19695pt}}_{{\kern-25.44746pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.01288pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2842pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 15.19695pt}}})\geq\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}](t)
.
∎

Theorem 4
.

In each consumption MDP
\mathcal{C}
there is a memoryless strategy
\sigma
such
that
\sigma\!\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}]_{\mathcal{C}}}}}}\models^{{\kern-22.37064pt{\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}]_{\mathcal{C}}}\kern 7.52623pt{\!}}}_{{\kern-7.13734pt\kern 7.52623pt{\mathcal{C}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}]_{\mathcal{C}}}}}}\models^{{\kern-22.37064pt{\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}]_{\mathcal{C}}}\kern 7.52623pt{\!}}}_{{\kern-7.13734pt\kern 7.52623pt{\mathcal{C}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}]_{\mathcal{C}}}}}}\models^{{\kern-17.45116pt{\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}]_{\mathcal{C}}}\kern 5.95122pt{\!}}}_{{\kern-5.56233pt\kern 5.95122pt{\mathcal{C}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}]_{\mathcal{C}}}}}}\models^{{\kern-16.40117pt{\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}]_{\mathcal{C}}}\kern 4.90123pt{\!}}}_{{\kern-4.51234pt\kern 4.90123pt{\mathcal{C}}}}}\!\mathsf{S}
. Moreover,

\sigma
can be computed in time polynomial with respect to the representation
of
\mathcal{C}
.

Proof.

Using
Lemma

2
, the existence of a memoryless strategy
follows from the fact that a strategy that fixes one min-safe action in each
state is safe. The complexity follows from
Theorem

3
.
∎

V
Positive reachability

In this section, we present the solution of the problem
called positive reachability. We focus on strategies that, given a set

T\subseteq S
of target states, safely satisfy

\mathsf{R}_{T}\subseteq\mathsf{S}
with positive probability. The main
contribution of this section is Algorithm
3
that computes

\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{R}]^{>0}
and the corresponding witness strategy. As before,
for the rest of this section we fix a CMDP
\mathcal{C}=(S,A,\Delta,\gamma,\mathit{R},\mathit{cap})
and also a set
T\subseteq S
.

Let
s\in S\smallsetminus T
be a state, let
d
be an initial load
and let
\sigma
be a strategy such that

\sigma\!\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{{s}}}}\models^{{\kern-10.18773pt{d}\kern 7.52623pt{\!>0}}}_{{\kern-9.9342pt{s}\kern 7.52623pt{}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{{s}}}}\models^{{\kern-10.18773pt{d}\kern 7.52623pt{\!>0}}}_{{\kern-9.9342pt{s}\kern 7.52623pt{}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{{s}}}}\models^{{\kern-7.88403pt{d}\kern 5.95122pt{\!>0}}}_{{\kern-7.70296pt{s}\kern 5.95122pt{}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{{s}}}}\models^{{\kern-6.83405pt{d}\kern 4.90123pt{\!>0}}}_{{\kern-6.65297pt{s}\kern 4.90123pt{}}}}\!\mathsf{R}_{T}
. Intuitively, as

\sigma\!\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{{s}}}}\models^{{\kern-10.18773pt{d}\kern 7.52623pt{\!>0}}}_{{\kern-9.9342pt{s}\kern 7.52623pt{}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{{s}}}}\models^{{\kern-10.18773pt{d}\kern 7.52623pt{\!>0}}}_{{\kern-9.9342pt{s}\kern 7.52623pt{}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{{s}}}}\models^{{\kern-7.88403pt{d}\kern 5.95122pt{\!>0}}}_{{\kern-7.70296pt{s}\kern 5.95122pt{}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{{s}}}}\models^{{\kern-6.83405pt{d}\kern 4.90123pt{\!>0}}}_{{\kern-6.65297pt{s}\kern 4.90123pt{}}}}\!\mathsf{R}
implies

\sigma\!\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{{s}}}}\models^{{\kern-10.18773pt{d}\kern 7.52623pt{\!}}}_{{\kern-9.9342pt{s}\kern 7.52623pt{}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{{s}}}}\models^{{\kern-10.18773pt{d}\kern 7.52623pt{\!}}}_{{\kern-9.9342pt{s}\kern 7.52623pt{}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{{s}}}}\models^{{\kern-7.88403pt{d}\kern 5.95122pt{\!}}}_{{\kern-7.70296pt{s}\kern 5.95122pt{}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{{s}}}}\models^{{\kern-6.83405pt{d}\kern 4.90123pt{\!}}}_{{\kern-6.65297pt{s}\kern 4.90123pt{}}}}\!\mathsf{S}
, the strategy is limited to safe actions.
For the reachability part,
\sigma
must start with an action

a=\sigma(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}s^{{\kern-6.43582pt{d}\kern 3.77432pt}}_{{\kern-12.04666pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.77432pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}s^{{\kern-6.43582pt{d}\kern 3.77432pt}}_{{\kern-12.04666pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.77432pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}s^{{\kern-4.22968pt{d}\kern 2.29686pt}}_{{\kern-8.23741pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.29686pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}s^{{\kern-3.57344pt{d}\kern 1.64063pt}}_{{\kern-7.58118pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.64063pt}}})
such that for at least successor
s^{\prime}
of
a
in

s
it holds that
\sigma\!\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{d^{\prime}}}}_{{{s^{\prime}}}}}\models^{{\kern-11.65022pt{d^{\prime}}\kern 7.52623pt{\!>0}}}_{{\kern-11.3967pt{s^{\prime}}\kern 7.52623pt{}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d^{\prime}}}}_{{{s^{\prime}}}}}\models^{{\kern-11.65022pt{d^{\prime}}\kern 7.52623pt{\!>0}}}_{{\kern-11.3967pt{s^{\prime}}\kern 7.52623pt{}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d^{\prime}}}}_{{{s^{\prime}}}}}\models^{{\kern-9.34653pt{d^{\prime}}\kern 5.95122pt{\!>0}}}_{{\kern-9.16545pt{s^{\prime}}\kern 5.95122pt{}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d^{\prime}}}}_{{{s^{\prime}}}}}\models^{{\kern-8.29654pt{d^{\prime}}\kern 4.90123pt{\!>0}}}_{{\kern-8.11546pt{s^{\prime}}\kern 4.90123pt{}}}}\!\mathsf{R}
with
d^{\prime}=d-\gamma(s,a)
. It must then continue in a similar fashion from
s^{\prime}
until either

T
is reached (and
\sigma
produces the desired run from

\mathsf{R}_{T}
) or until there is no such action.

To formalize the intuition, we define two auxiliary functions. Let us fix a
state
s
, and action
a
, a successor
s^{\prime}\in\mathit{Succ}(s,a)
, and a vector

\mathbf{x}\in\overline{\mathbb{N}}^{S}
. We define the
hope value
of

s^{\prime}
for
a
in
s
based on
\mathbf{x}
, denoted by
\mathit{HV}(\mathbf{x},s,a,s^{\prime})
, and the
safe value
of
a
in
s
based on
\mathbf{x}
, denoted by
\mathit{SV}(\mathbf{x},s,a)
, as follows.

\displaystyle\mathit{HV}(\mathbf{x},s,a,s^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}t\in\mathit{Succ}(s,a)\\
t\neq s^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\{\mathbf{x}(s^{\prime}),\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}](t)\}

\displaystyle\mathit{SV}(\mathbf{x},s,a)

\displaystyle=\gamma(s,a)+\min_{s^{\prime}\in\mathit{Succ}(s,a)}\mathit{HV}(\mathbf{x},s,a,s^{\prime})

The hope value of
s^{\prime}
for
a
in
s
represents the lowest level of resource
that the agent needs to have after playing
a
in order to (i) have at least

\mathbf{x}(s^{\prime})
units of resource if the outcome of
a
is
s^{\prime}
, and
(ii) to survive otherwise.

We again use functionals to iteratively compute
\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{R}^{i}]^{>0}
,
with a fixed point equal to
\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{R}]^{>0}
. The main operator

\mathcal{B}
just applies
\llbracket\,\cdot\,\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}^{\mathit{cap}}
on the result of the
auxiliary functional
\mathcal{A}
. The application of

\llbracket\,\cdot\,\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}^{\mathit{cap}}
ensures that whenever the result is higher than

\mathit{cap}
, it set to
\infty
, and that in reload states the value is either
0

or
\infty
, which is in line what is expected from
\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{R}]^{>0}
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}(\mathbf{x})(s)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}](s)&\text{if }s\in T\\
\min_{a\in A}\mathit{SV}(\mathbf{x},s,a)&\text{otherwise};\end{cases}

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\llbracket\,\mathcal{A}(\mathbf{x})\,\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}^{\mathit{cap}}

By
\mathbf{y}_{T}
we denote a vector such that

\mathbf{y}_{T}(s)=\begin{cases}\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}](s)&\text{if }s\in T\\
\infty&\text{if }s\not\in T.\end{cases}

The following two lemmata relate
\mathcal{B}
to
\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{R}]^{>0}

and show that
\mathcal{B}
applied iteratively on
\mathbf{y}_{T}
reaches
fixed point in a number of iterations that is polynomial with respect to the representation of
\mathcal{C}
. Their proofs are quite
straightforward but technical, hence we moved them to Appendix.

Lemma 3
.

Consider the iteration of
\mathcal{B}
on the initial vector

\mathbf{y}_{T}
. Then for each
i\geq 0
it holds that

\mathcal{B}^{i}(\mathbf{y}_{T})=\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{R}^{i}]^{>0}
.

Lemma 4
.

Let
K=|\mathit{R}|+(|\mathit{R}|+1)\cdot(|S|-|\mathit{R}|+1)
. Then

\mathcal{B}^{K}(\mathbf{y}_{T})=\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{R}]^{>0}
.

Input:

CMDP
\mathcal{C}=(S,A,\Delta,\gamma,\mathit{R},\mathit{cap})
and

T\subseteq S

Output:

The vector
\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{R}_{T}]_{\mathcal{C}}^{>0}
, rule selector

\Phi

compute
\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}]
;

/*
Algorithm

2
*/

foreach

s\in S

do

1

\Phi(s)(\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}](s))\leftarrow\text{arbitrary min-safe{} action of }s

\mathbf{p}\leftarrow\{\infty\}^{S}
;

2

foreach

t\in T

do

\mathbf{p}(t)\leftarrow\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}](t)
;

3

repeat

4

\mathbf{p}_{\mathit{old}}\leftarrow\mathbf{p}
;

5

foreach

s\in S\smallsetminus T

do

6

\mathbf{a}(s)\leftarrow\arg\min_{a\in A}\mathit{SV}(\mathbf{p}_{\mathit{old}},s,a)
;

7

\mathbf{p}(s)\leftarrow\min_{a\in A}\mathit{SV}(\mathbf{p}_{\mathit{old}},s,a)
;

8

\mathbf{p}\leftarrow\llbracket\,\mathbf{p}\,\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}^{\mathit{cap}}
;

9

foreach

s\in S\smallsetminus T

do

10

if

\mathbf{p}(s)<\mathbf{p}_{\mathit{old}}(s)

then

11

\Phi(s)(\mathbf{p}(s))\leftarrow\mathbf{a}(s)
;

12

until

\,\mathbf{p}_{\mathit{old}}=\mathbf{p}
;

13

return

\mathbf{p},\Phi
;

14

Algorithm 3

Computing
\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{R}_{T}]^{>0}
and a corresponding witness rule selector.

Algorithm

3
computes
\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{R}]^{>0}
and a corresponding
witness rule selector
\Phi
. On lines
3
and

3

\mathbf{p}
is initialized to
\mathbf{y}_{T}
. The
repeat-loop on
Algorithms

3
to
3
iterates

\mathcal{B}
on
\mathbf{p}
(and
\mathbf{p}_{\mathit{old}}
) and builds the witness
selector gradually. In particular, the
Algorithm

3
stores the
application of
\mathcal{A}
on
\mathbf{p}_{\mathit{old}}
in
\mathbf{p}
, the

Algorithm

3
performs
\mathcal{B}
, and the condition on

Algorithm

3
checks for the fixed point. Finally,
lines
3
and

3
-
3
update
\Phi
accordingly.
The correctness and complexity of the algorithm are stated formally in theorems
5
(
\mathbf{p}=\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{R}]^{>0}
) and
6
(correctness of
\Phi
).

Example 5
.

Consider again
Fig.

4
which shows the values of

\mathbf{p1}
: the vector
\mathbf{p}
after one iteration of the repeat loop of

Algorithm

3
. In this iteration, the algorithm set
\Phi
to play

{\color[rgb]{0,0.8,0.8}\mathsf{b}}
in
s
with resource level
10
or more. The final values of
\mathbf{p}=\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{R}]^{>0}
computed by
Algorithm

3
are equal to

\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}]
for this example (as we can safely reach
t
from all
reloads). In the iteration, where
\mathbf{p}(s)=2
for the first
time, the selector is updated to play
{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0.8}\mathsf{a}}
in
s
with resource level
between
2
and
10
(excluded). Note that the computed
\Phi
exactly
matches the one mentioned in
Example

3
.

Theorem 5
.

Algorithm

3
always terminates after a number of steps that is
polynomial with respect to the representation of
\mathcal{C}
, and upon termination,

\mathbf{p}=\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{R}]^{>0}
.

Proof.

The complexity part follows from
Lemma

4
and the fact that
each iteration takes only linear number of steps. The correctness part is an
immediate corollary of
Lemma

4
and the fact that

Algorithm

3
iterates
\mathcal{B}
on
\mathbf{y}_{T}
until a
fixed point.
∎

Theorem 6
.

Upon termination of
Algorithm

3
, the computed rule selector

\Phi
encodes a strategy
\sigma_{\Phi}
such that

\sigma_{\Phi}\!\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathbf{v}}}}}\models^{{\kern-10.61134pt{\mathbf{v}}\kern 7.52623pt{\!>0}}}_{{\kern-7.13734pt\kern 7.52623pt{}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathbf{v}}}}}\models^{{\kern-10.61134pt{\mathbf{v}}\kern 7.52623pt{\!>0}}}_{{\kern-7.13734pt\kern 7.52623pt{}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathbf{v}}}}}\models^{{\kern-8.18661pt{\mathbf{v}}\kern 5.95122pt{\!>0}}}_{{\kern-5.56233pt\kern 5.95122pt{}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathbf{v}}}}}\models^{{\kern-7.13663pt{\mathbf{v}}\kern 4.90123pt{\!>0}}}_{{\kern-4.51234pt\kern 4.90123pt{}}}}\!\mathsf{R}
for

\mathbf{v}=\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{R}]^{>0}
. As a consequence, a polynomial-size
finite counter strategy for the positive reachability problem can be computed in
time polynomial with respect to representation of
\mathcal{C}
.

Proof.

The complexity follows from
Theorem

5
. Indeed, since the
algorithm has a polynomial complexity, also the size of
\Phi
is
polynomial. The correctness proof is based on the following invariant of the
main repeat-loop. The vector
\mathbf{p}
and the finite counter strategy
\pi=\sigma_{\Phi}
have these properties:

(a)

It holds that
\mathbf{p}\geq\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}]
.

(b)

Strategy
\pi
is safe.

(c)

For each
s\in S
with
d
such that
\mathbf{p}(s)\leq d\leq\mathit{cap}
, there is a finite
\pi
-compatible path

\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\alpha^{{\kern-7.86026pt{d}\kern 5.19876pt}}_{{\kern-13.4711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.19876pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\alpha^{{\kern-7.86026pt{d}\kern 5.19876pt}}_{{\kern-13.4711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.19876pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\alpha^{{\kern-5.06735pt{d}\kern 3.13454pt}}_{{\kern-9.07509pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.13454pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\alpha^{{\kern-4.17177pt{d}\kern 2.23895pt}}_{{\kern-8.1795pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.23895pt}}}=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}s^{{\kern-6.43582pt{d}\kern 3.77432pt}}_{{\kern-12.04666pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern with

and

\mathit{RL}(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\alpha^{{\kern-7.86026pt{d}\kern 5.19876pt}}_{{\kern-13.4711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.19876pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\alpha^{{\kern-7.86026pt{d}\kern 5.19876pt}}_{{\kern-13.4711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.19876pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\alpha^{{\kern-5.06735pt{d}\kern 3.13454pt}}_{{\kern-9.07509pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.13454pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\alpha^{{\kern-4.17177pt{d}\kern 2.23895pt}}_{{\kern-8.1795pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.23895pt}}})=r_{1},r_{2},\ldots,r_{n}
never
drops below
\mathbf{p}
, which is
r_{i}\geq\mathbf{p}(s_{i})
for all

.

The theorem then follows from (
b
) and
(
c
) of this invariant and from

Theorem

5
.

Clearly, all parts of the invariants hold after the initialization on

Algorithms

3
to
3
. The first item of the
invariant follows from the definition of
\mathit{SV}
and
\mathit{HV}
. In
particular, if
\mathbf{p_{\mathit{old}}}\geq\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}]
, then

\mathit{SV}(\mathbf{p_{\mathit{old}}},s,a)\geq\mathit{AV}(\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}],s,a)\geq\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}](s)
for all

and
a
. The part
(

) follows from (
a
), as the action
assigned to
\Phi
on
Algorithm

3
is safe for
s
with

\mathbf{p}(s)
units of resource (again, due to
\mathbf{p}(s)=\mathit{SV}(\mathbf{p_{\mathit{old}}},s,a)\geq\mathit{AV}(\mathbf{ml}[\mathsf{S}],s,a)
); hence, only
actions that are safe for the corresponding state and resource level are
assigned to
\Phi
. By

2
,
\pi
is safe.

The proof of (

) is more involved. Assume that an
iteration of the main repeat loop was performed. Denote by
\pi_{\mathit{old}}
the
strategy encoded by
\mathbf{p}
and
\Phi
from the previous iteration. Let

be any state such that
\mathbf{p}(s)\leq\mathit{cap}
. If

\mathbf{p}(s)=\mathbf{p}_{\mathit{old}}(s)
, then

) follows directly from the induction hypothesis: for
each state

,

was only redefined for values smaller then

\mathbf{p}_{\mathit{old}}(q)
and thus the history witnessing
(

) for
\pi_{\mathit{old}}
is also
\pi
-compatible.

The case where
\mathbf{p}(s)<\mathbf{p}_{\mathit{old}}(s)
is treated

selected on

and assigned to

for

\mathbf{p}(s)
on line

. By definition of

, there must be

such that

\mathit{HV}(\mathbf{p_{\mathit{old}}},s,a,t)+\gamma(s,a)=\mathit{SV}(\mathbf{p_{\mathit{old}}},s,a)
(which
is equal to
\mathbf{p}(s)
before the truncation on

). In particular, it holds that
l=\mathit{last}\mathit{RL}(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathbf{p}(s)}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[15.05212pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.05212pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.23848pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.59895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}sat^{{\kern-20.4824pt{\mathbf{p}(s)}\kern 11.13281pt}}_{{\kern-40.80708pt\mathchoice{\makebox[15.05212pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.05212pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.23848pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.59895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 11.13281pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathbf{p}(s)}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[15.05212pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.05212pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.23848pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.59895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}sat^{{\kern-20.4824pt{\mathbf{p}(s)}\kern 11.13281pt}}_{{\kern-40.80708pt\mathchoice{\makebox[15.05212pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.05212pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.23848pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.59895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern (even
after the truncation). Then, by the induction hypothesis, there is a

) for

\pi_{\mathit{old}}
. Then, the loaded history

\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathbf{p}(s)}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[15.05212pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.05212pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.23848pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.59895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\alpha^{{\kern-14.54836pt{\mathbf{p}(s)}\kern 5.19876pt}}_{{\kern-34.87303pt\mathchoice{\makebox[15.05212pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.05212pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.23848pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.59895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.19876pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathbf{p}(s)}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[15.05212pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.05212pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.23848pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.59895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\alpha^{{\kern-14.54836pt{\mathbf{p}(s)}\kern 5.19876pt}}_{{\kern-34.87303pt\mathchoice{\makebox[15.05212pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.05212pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.23848pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.59895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.19876pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathbf{p}(s)}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[15.05212pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.05212pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.23848pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.59895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\alpha^{{\kern-9.8446pt{\mathbf{p}(s)}\kern 3.13454pt}}_{{\kern-24.36226pt\mathchoice{\makebox[15.05212pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.05212pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.23848pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.59895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.13454pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathbf{p}(s)}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[15.05212pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.05212pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.23848pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.59895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\alpha^{{\kern-8.94902pt{\mathbf{p}(s)}\kern 2.23895pt}}_{{\kern-23.46667pt\mathchoice{\makebox[15.05212pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.05212pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.23848pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.59895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.23895pt}}}
with
\alpha=sat\odot\beta
is
(i) compatible with
\pi
and, moreover, (ii) we have that

\mathit{RL}(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathbf{p_{\mathit{old}}}(s)}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[21.81319pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[21.81319pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[14.21068pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[11.57115pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\alpha^{{\kern-19.52055pt{\mathbf{p_{\mathit{old}}}(s)}\kern 5.19876pt}}_{{\kern-51.89955pt\mathchoice{\makebox[21.81319pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[21.81319pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[14.21068pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[11.57115pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.13454pt}}_{{\kern-37.0955pt\mathchoice{\makebox[21.81319pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[21.81319pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[14.21068pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[11.57115pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern never drops below

.
Indeed, (i)

(see

), and (ii)
\Phi
was only redefined for values

\mathbf{p}_{\mathit{old}}
and thus
\pi
mimics
\pi_{\mathit{old}}
from
t
onward.
For the initial load
\mathbf{p}(s)<d^{\prime}\leq\mathit{cap}
the same arguments
apply. This finishes the proof of the invariant and thus also the proof of
